## Grand Prix Nagoya 2012

  
![](https://i.ormos.uk/86/da/86da31b791854b4e8a9fe5ccbd4b075f3351c5a4466468a7e19af9fa8fb21944.jpg)

Congratulations to Yuuji Okita, the Grand Prix Nagoya 2012 Champion! Despite reservations about his matchup with the ubiquitous Zombies, Okita managed to run the tables to a perfect 9-0, before dispatching both RB Aggro and a BR Zombies deck on his way to the title. Okita's win prevents Zombies from taking its third title in four events, something that is sure to cause celebrations around the world.

In addition to this triumph over the dark armies, Grand Prix Nagoya provided us a great look at how the metagame of Standard continues to evolve. GW Aggro has died, UW Control has added red, Zombies is packing heat for Zombies, and Reanimator has become a major force. The real question at this point is how will things change between now and Grand Prix Atlantic City next month? A month is a long time to wait, but when it finally gets here, you can be sure that players will have paid close attention to what happened this weekend.

Until then, congratulations once again to Yuuji Okita and the Human race for surviving the Zombie apocalypse to become the Grand Prix Nagoya 2012 Champion!

## Sunday, 4:40 p.m. – Deck Tech: Red Black Aggro with Kazumasa Satou

*by Steve Sadin*

 

Before this weekend, if I had heard that someone was playing an aggressive deck with [Swamp](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Swamp)s and [Mountain](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mountain)s, I would have assumed that they were playing Black-Red Zombies. But after seeing the many types of Aggressive Red Black decks that players have found success with this weekend, I won't be so close-minded.

Turns out, there are a number of very different ways to build aggressive [Blood Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Crypt) decks. One way to do it is to switch over to a red base and to fill up the earliest parts of your curve with cards like [Stromkirk Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stromkirk+Noble), [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler), and [Ash Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ash+Zealot).

This is exactly what Kazumasa Satou did, and he was rewarded with a Top 8 finish for his resourcefulness.

**Why did you play your Red Black deck instead of Zombies?**

"My deck plays a lot like Zombies against [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) decks, but my deck is very good against Black Red Zombies. Because of cards like [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul), [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), and Geralf's Messenger, Black-Red zombies is very weak on defense – while I get to control the race by playing things like [Ash Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ash+Zealot) which is a very good blocker, and a good attacker."

**Why did you play [Rakdos Keyrune](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Keyrune)?** 

"It avoids [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict), it gives you defesnse against [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk), it can't be killed by [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price) or sorcery speed removal, and if you have a [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) it can attack past [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel). Plus, turn three [Rakdos Keyrune](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Keyrune), fourth turn [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite) is nuts."

**Why did you play [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price)?** 

"Because it kills [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) by itself –and [Victim of Night](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Victim+of+Night) is too difficult for my deck to cast."

**Why did you play [Mizzium Mortars](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mizzium+Mortars)?** 

"Like [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price), it's removal that can kill [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) – but it can also kill [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter). Plus, sometimes I can jump my mana up to six thanks to [Rakdos Keyrune](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Keyrune) and overload it which is obviously very good."

**Does you deck have any bad matchups?**

"Naya. [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) plus [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) is very hard for me to beat, and [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) is a very big problem. My friend Isao Kimata lost three times to Naya yesterday, while my only loss came against Jund because of mulligan troubles."

**Why did you play [Archwing Dragon](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Archwing+Dragon) in your sideboard?** 

"I have it against Blue White Flash. They can't block with their [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel)s, I can make it uncounterable with [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls), and they can't kill it with [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict)."

**Would you play this deck at a tournament next weekend?**

"Definitely!"

* * *

## Sunday, 5:00 p.m. – Deck Tech: Human Reanimator with Yuji Okita

*by Nate Price*

 

![Unburial Rites](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Unburial+Rites)

Ironically, Okita admitted that he didn't feel his deck had a very good matchup with Zombies, especially before sideboarding.

"I do not really like the matchup with Zombies," he said. "This deck is a combo deck, and that matchup is a classic combo versus control match. It is a race. They are trying to kill me, and I am trying to flashback [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) on an [Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise) on turn four. I don't have nearly as much control as I do in other matchups."

This struck me as odd, especially considering the power of [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler) and [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) to dominate creature matchups. The Staticaster alone is capable of blocking almost every creature the Zombies deck has and killing a few of them to boot. Okita explained this disparity to me.

"It is because of their removal," he explained. "They are playing more removal spells to beat the creature decks, and they can use it to kill my creatures, too."

It seems like this is another case of being a casualty of the response to Zombies. Still, this could have some very positive repercussions in weeks to come. If trends continue, it seems that many of the more aggressive decks are becoming more like midrange decks. A good example of this can be seen in the strong showing of Naya decks in this tournament. As that happens, Human Reanimator could see a very conducive format emerge.

"My best matchup is definitely against GW decks. Decks like GW, Naya, and other midrange decks are very weak against Staticaster and Peddler, giving me an advantage. I don't think I would play this deck next week, but I can see a time when this deck might be best."

When it does find that perfect window, it will be interesting to see what wrinkles have been added. The deck's construction is very straightforward. One-third of the deck is creatures, one-third of the deck is cards that put cards into the graveyard, and one-third of the deck is lands. Toss four copies of [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) into this and you have probably the most straightforward deck concept in Standard. Straightforward decks like this can be hard to alter, as they tend to be very tight and require every card that is already a part of the deck. Still, it never hurts to look for ways to improve upon things.

"I would definitely change the mana," Okita said after deliberation. "Right now, I have no black mana sources, so I cannot cast [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) without flashing it back. This has been problematic at times, so I would like to make that easier on me."

![Chronic Flooding](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Chronic+Flooding)

I honestly hadn't noticed the lack of black mana and how ridiculous that was with [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) being so important to the deck until Okita mentioned it. After all, he never seemed to me to have trouble casting it. Much of that comes from the very impressive suite of cards aimed at filling up his graveyard. In addition to the expected [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch), [Tracker](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tracker)'s Instinct, and [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting), Okita has two absolute gems rounding things out: [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) and [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm).

"Ideally, I want to cast [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch) on turn two and [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) on turn three. [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) is possibly the best card in my deck," he beamed as he picked a copy up.

It's easy to see why. I have literally never seen anyone play that card, even in Limited. The fact that it was not only being played in Standard, but contending for Top 8, absolutely thrilled me. It is the single most efficient mill spell in his entire deck, able to hit at least three times most games, netting him nine cards for a mere two mana and one card. If he gets two out, I have watched Okita put half of his deck into his graveyard before turn six. In a deck that wants to combo out as fast as possible, this depth of digging is invaluable. Nearly as impressive, especially given the field, is [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm).

"[Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm) is very good against aggro decks," Okita said. "I have two more in the sideboard that I bring in against them. It can also be good against control."

It is always great when a deck wants to use all three abilities on a Charm as much as Human Reanimator does [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm). First, there is the obvious ability to use it as a discard enabler. Add to that the ability to function as a two-mana, instant-speed removal spell, and you have a potent card against aggressive decks. Finally, the ability to counter counterspells against control really makes this a perfect card for this deck. It is likely that Okita should have been playing a full four in his main deck instead of leaving two in the sideboard.

Despite Okita's initial comments to the contrary, he did admit that his matchup against aggressive decks got better after the first game.

"I have two [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm)s to bring in against Zombies. I also have these," indicating four [Cathedral Sanctifier](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cathedral+Sanctifier)s.

![Gravecrawler](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Gravecrawler)

Harkening back to the Human Reanimator deck that gained popularity during *Innistrad*-block Constructed, the Sanctifier is ideal in the matchup with Zombies. It gives a great life boost. The 1/1 body is capable of either trading with [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler)s to slow opponents down or blocking a [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger), preventing it from dying and retriggering the ETB effect. All of this is designed to buy time until an [Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise) comes into play to allow it to all happen again. He also has [Rolling Temblor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rolling+Temblor), which functions as an effective board-clearing spell against Zombie decks. The remainder of the cards in his sideboard are great for making his matchup with control better.

"Against control, I can't rely on [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites)," he told me. "They have [Rest in Peace](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rest+in+Peace), which can be very tough for me to handle. I have [Ray of Revelation](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ray+of+Revelation), which can get rid of it, but my best plan is just to be aggressive and attack them. I can use my [Cavern of Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cavern+of+Souls) to force [Goldnight Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldnight+Commander)s, [Geist-Honored Monk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geist-Honored+Monk), [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells), and eventually [Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise) into play and attack them. If they don't have [Rest in Peace](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rest+in+Peace), I don't care as much about cards like [Supreme Verdict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Supreme+Verdict). I can just make an Angel and use it to kill them in one attack with Izzet [Snap](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snap)caster and [Goldnight Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldnight+Commander)s."

Currently, it seems like this deck might not be positioned the best, especially if Okita's pessimism about the matchup against Zombies is to be believed. If that is possible to overcome, which it may very well be, this version of Reanimator provides a much more explosive option than the Junk version.

So there you have it. You have two extremes, both operating under the same mechanical umbrella. You have Junk on one side, with its drawn-out, grinding efficiency and consistency. You have Human on the other side, with its single-minded focus and explosiveness. Which you choose is up to you. If nothing else, the fact that both decks were in Top 8 contention after sixteen rounds of play proves that there is more than one way to skin a cat. Or reanimate an Angel. Whichever metaphor floats your boat.

#### Okita, Yuji

#####

* * *

## Quarterfinals – Mamoru Nagai (Naya) vs. Kazumasa Satou (RB Aggro)

*by Nate Price*

 

In a format of players trying to "go over the top" of one another, Kazumasa Satou has chosen to take the low road, going under them. His RB Aggro deck is the natural successor to the Monored decks of earlier in the season. Taking advantage of the naturally faster backbone that the Monored deck has to offer, Satou has added [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat), [Ultimate Price](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ultimate+Price), and [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) to the deck to give it a bit more ability to clean up after his early damage.

His opponent this round is Pro Tour *Dark Ascension* Top 8 competitor Mamoru Nagai, running Naya. Naya is positioned very well in this tournament, taking advantage of the leagues of aggressive creatures running around the format by playing cards like [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned), [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells), and [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk).

As the higher seed after the Swiss, Nagai earned the right to choose to play first. He mulliganned his first hand, but seemed more or less content to stay with his six-card hand. He was going to need every edge his deck could provide him, as Satou's deck was capable of some blazing starts. First came [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler), followed immediately by [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy). Nagai merely built his mana. Satou added a [Rakdos Keyrune](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Keyrune) on his third turn, one of the silent stars of his deck.

Come the fourth turn, Nagai had been knocked down to 13 before he began to build himself back up. A [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) came down, bringing with it a 2/2 Wolf token and 2 life. Satou came prepared for the inevitable meeting with Huntmaster, however, using a [Mizzium Mortars](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mizzium+Mortars) to remove it before activating his Keyrune and attacking with his team. Nagai was forced to trade his Wolf for the Cackler, dropping back down to 10. [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) was a miracle topdeck for Nagai, allowing him to remove the [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), but the Keyrune was unfazed.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ad/0f/ad0f33e7db0c6f84a648325c606348ab4a95de47bd656a9e408a6a230fc6de6a.jpg)

In addition to providing a three-powered, first-striking attacker, the Keyrune also provides the RB deck with a reliable way to make sure that it is able to hit five mana, essential for hitting the top end of its curve. With all of the pieces in place, Satou was able to cast a [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite), getting in for a quick five damage, halving Nagai's life total. After Nagai passed the turn, Satou added an [Ash Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ash+Zealot) to his side and attacked. Nagai had one of the few cards in the format that is capable of reliably killing the Hellkite, [Selesnya Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Selesnya+Charm), but the two damage from the Zealot was enough to put Nagai in range of [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear), giving the first game to Satou.

Mamoru Nagai 0 - Kazumasa Satou 1

Nagai's second game began much better than his first. Off the back of a first-turn [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim), he was able to put a brick wall into play before Satou could even get started. [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter) is a massive obstacle for the BR deck to deal with, especially if they don't have [Mizzium Mortars](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mizzium+Mortars) to remove it.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/c7/31/c73157bb04245186c26c8c3095159c8dc9dda4adedcf02dbf24499ea00008b8b.jpg)

Satou had a [Stromkirk Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stromkirk+Noble) on his first turn, but it was forced to sit around doing nothing until the [Smite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smite)r got out of the way. While waiting for that to happen, Satou aimed a pair of [Appetite for Brains](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Appetite+for+Brains) at Nagai, revealing:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/2e/8e/2e8e74955c07ea7ea1761c637a156ce27637dea97b1418674dd1f95b4d33b1f6.jpg)

Quickly putting the two Huntmasters into the exiled zone, Nagai shrugged and went to take his turn. With no other business in his hand, he simply chose to attack with his [Smite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smite)r and use his [Kessig Wolf Run](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?&name=Kessig%2BWolf%2BRun) to pump it up. Satou took advantage of the window to attack with his Noble, turning it into a 2/2. Over the next two turns, Satou added a pair of Knights of Infamy to his side, giving him a pair of attackers that could get through Nagai's defenses.

Unfortunately for Satou, one of Nagai's defenses was also one of his biggest threats: [Sigarda, Host of Herons](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sigarda%2C+Host+of+Herons). As soon as she hit play, you could see that Satou was nervous. He hadn't been able to get off the ground incredibly fast, and was facing down a ridiculously fast clock. He tried to get through for as much as he could with his protection from white, but it didn't take long before Nagai sealed things with a [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) and [Kessig Wolf Run](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Kessig+Wolf+Run), still sitting at an unfairly safe life total.

Mamoru Nagai 1 - Kazumasa Satou 1

For the final game, Satou was on the play, which greatly benefitted his incredibly aggressive deck. He opened with a [Stromkirk Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stromkirk+Noble) and [Ash Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ash+Zealot), quickly dropping Nagai to 17. Just as in the previous game, Nagai made an early [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter) thanks to an [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim). And, just as in the previous game, Satou was stopped cold.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/03/c2/03c2f015616a61ace1aebeed1023521fa184410a23dfcb4407517dc7d86f5245.jpg)

Satou took a moment to write the cards down before considering his options. Both the [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) and Huntmaster were bad for his deck, providing effectively two creatures and a reasonable amount of tempo loss. Even though the Huntmaster was going to be able to hit play faster, Satou chose to take the [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk), which effectively gives the same benefits but on a larger scale.

As expected, Nagai untapped and made the Huntmaster that Satou had left him. This drove his life total back up to 19, far from where Satou would like to see it. A [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) provided the first opportunity to put Nagai back down to a manageable level, but 15 was still a few turns from closure. Plus, Nagai had a reasonable army of his own. [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter) was in a position to attack now, flanked by Huntmaster and his Wolf, so Nagai began to attack. With the seeming board advantage, Nagai pressed harder, passing the turn to flip his Huntmaster. Nagai targeted the Aristocrat with the Ravager's triggered ability, causing Satou to sacrifice his [Ash Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ash+Zealot).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/26/16/261695138a8d5cb49594c17ea1d9a0a7d0aa1ecf0566d3a3ff7f3a76049b8a2d.jpg)

Facing a difficult situation, Satou understandably took his time to figure out what to do. After about forty-five seconds of deliberation, Satou added an Ashe Zealot to his team and attacked with it and the Aristocrat into a 2/2 Wolf, [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim), and [Ravager of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravager+of+the+Fells). When Nagai blocked the Zealot with his Ravager, Satou tried to finish it with a [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear) after resolving first-strike damage. In response to that, however, Nagai had the [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) needed to save it, generating a new Wolf and 2 life in the process.

In the face of this ridiculous turnaround and already desperate odds, Satou nodded and conceded the match to Nagai.

**Mamoru Nagai 2 - Kazumasa Satou 1**

* * *

## Quarterfinals – Kenji Tsumura (Junk Reanimator) vs Yuuji Okita (Human Reanimator)

*by Steve Sadin*

 

Hall of Famer, and 6 time Pro Tour Top 8 Competitor Kenji Tsumura spent the better part of the last two weeks playtesting his Green White Black Reanimator deck to a point where he felt completely comfortable playing it this weekend. He even cut the [Lingering Souls](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Lingering+Souls) (a card which had previously been thought to be an automatic inclusion) from the deck because they were underperforming against a field full of [Thundermaw Hellkite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thundermaw+Hellkite)s.

His hard work has once again paid off for him, as he finds himself in the thirteenth Grand Prix Top 8 of his career.

But despite his plentiful time spent practicing, Tsumura now finds himself playing against a deck that he had not expected to see this weekend...

Yuuji Okita decided to play a Human Reanimator deck that uses [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) (yes, really) as one of his primary ways to fill up his graveyard with humans like [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler), [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster), [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) and [Goldnight Commander](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Goldnight+Commander) which he brings back with Angel of Glory's Rise.

While the deck is loaded up with cards that might cause players to laugh on first sight, Okita has proven this weekend that the deck is incredibly potent.

### Game One

Tsumura opened with an Avacyn's Pilgrim, and a pair of [Centaur Healer](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Centaur+Healer)s – but those would ultimately prove to be of little significance against Okita's combo of [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler), and [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) quickly killed them all off.

Okita then further built up his board with another [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster), and filled his graveyard with a [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch) - while Tsumura rapidly dug through his own deck with a pair of [Grisly Salvage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grisly+Salvage)s, and a [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch).

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e1/0c/e10ca814c9feddeea7a30affc687fcec7ee97d73394dea35de46e9f9f3f47851.jpg)

Before Kenji could set up anything of note, however, Okita cast an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) on an Angel of Glory's Rise that brought a [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) along with it.

Realizing that he didn't have much time to act, Tsumura cast an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) to return his [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) into play. [Predict](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Predict)ably, Okita activated his [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) to kill off the [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) at the end of Tsumura's turn.

Okita then untapped and made exactly the play that Tsumura was hoping he would make – activating [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) on his main phase to kill a beast token, and set up an attack with Angel of Glory's Rise, [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) and a wolf.

This was just the opening that Kenji had been hoping for.

With the deathtouching [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) tapped, Tsumura used the opportunity to cast an [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity) – targeting Okita's [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler), and both of his [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster)s.

However, even this seemingly good opportunity wouldn't be enough for Kenji.

In response to the Angel's enter the battlefield trigger, Okita activated his untapped [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) to kill off his own [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler).

During Tsumura's end step, Okita played another [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster), then untapped and Cast an Angel of Glory's Rise which brought back a [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler).

A few quick attacks later, and Okita was up a game.

Yuuji Okita 1 – Kenji Tsumura 0

### Game Two

Both players mulliganed to begin game two, then spent their early turns filling up their graveyards.

Tsumura's [Grisly Salvage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Grisly+Salvage) put a [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity) right where he wanted it, while a pair of [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding)s, a [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch), an [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm) and a [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) gave Okita an abundance of tools to work with.

As soon as he got the opportunity, Tsumura cast an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) returning an [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity), and exiling an Angel of Glory's Rise, and two [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster)s from Okita's Graveyard

![](https://i.ormos.uk/73/f9/73f999901af848e6f05083dde3f4f8136e405993098ff9a9c384769f99f345fb.jpg)

However, that would only serve to slow Okita down a bit as he continued ripping through his deck with his two [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding)s.

Before Tsumura could so much as put a dent in Okita's life total, the rogue deckbuilder had cast an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) for an Angel of Glory's Rise that brough a [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler), and a [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) (which he immediately paired together) along with it.

At this point all that Kenji could do was cast a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) which, in the context of this game, served to do very little.

Okita then passed his turn without casting a spell, allowing his werewolf to flip into a deathtouching [Ravager of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravager+of+the+Fells) which killed off Tsumura's [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity).

Another [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) allowed Tsumura to bring back his [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity) – which came into play, and targeted Okita's Angel of Glory's Rise, [Ravager of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravager+of+the+Fells), and [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler).

But just like in the first game, Okita responded to the angel's trigger by casting an [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster), and killing off his own [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler).

A few more [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) triggers later, and Okita's graveyard had once again grown to mammoth sizes. When he was finally good and ready, Okita cast an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) for Angel of Glory's Rise that brought a [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells), an [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster), and three [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler)s back along with it.

Tsumura, fully aware of just how bad of a situation he was in, untapped and cast a [Sever the Bloodline](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Sever+the+Bloodline) targeting [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler) - but in response, Okita activated an [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) to kill off Kenji's [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity), then activated another [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) to kill all of his own [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler)s.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6f/a1/6fa12df633506c66e6cffce78284f2c33bd1eb73341df023e7386d1a5e83a331.jpg)

After the dust had settled, Tsumura tapped out to flashback an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) to targeting [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity), but Okita countered it with an [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm).

When Okita hard cast another Angel of Glory's Rise a turn later Tsumura knew he was beat.

After lingering over the board for a moment, Kenji extended his hand and wished Yuuji good luck in the Semifinals.

Yuuji Okita 2 – Kenji Tsumura 0

* * *

## Semifinals – Rahman Aryabhima vs Mamoru Nagai

*by Nate Price*

 

Coming into the Semifinals, Rahman Aryabhima found himself in position to join only Brian Kibler, Martin Juza, and Alex Shvartzman as the only non-Japanese players in the history of **Magic** to win a Grand Prix in Japan. His weapon of choice was an interestingly tweaked BR Zombies deck featuring [Blood Artist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Artist), [Mark of Mutiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mark+of+Mutiny), and [Flames of the Firebrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flames+of+the+Firebrand). All three of those cards are fairly good against the aggressive decks in the field, giving him quite an edge throughout the course of the tournament.

His opponent had a deck very well suited to the field as well. Mamoru Nagai's Naya deck has all of the tools needed to survive a Zombie apocalypse: ways to keep his health up, a little angelic help, and lots and lots of fire. In a format once declared impossible for midrange to succeed in, clearly Nagai and his Naya deck have proven them wrong.

Aryabhima got started quickly, as expected from his Zombies deck. [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) and [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) both hit play early and began to go to work on Nagai's life total. A temporary stumble kept Aryabhima from making his second land drop, but its appearance on the following turn allowed him to keep developing. A [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) joined the fray, and Nagai soon found himself down to 8 life and facing a trio of attackers.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/f7/d5/f7d5004bac797393f04ccd705a8315c014231df7c0ec73dacda9bf9c8ec7d468.jpg)

He gave himself some breathing room on the following turn, as a [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) for one cleared away everything but the [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul). Aryabhima was able to rebuild with a second Ghoul and a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) on his turn, dropping Nagai to 6. Nagai once again found a stabilizing influence, this time in the form of [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells). His life went back up to 8, and the two creatures provided him a perfect way to slow Aryabhima down.

Aryabhima attacked with his three creatures. Nagai wasted little time before blocking the [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) with his Wolf token. Aryabhima replaced it with another 2/2, this time an unleashed [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler). When Nagai made a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) on the following turn, it appeared that he might be able to push himself just outside of the Danger Zone.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/e8/28/e828823fe751c8942cb4d3e5cc945d672f906b24a6b4c0080dc5c9277fb26de6.jpg)

Aryabhima had different plans, however, using a [Mark of Mutiny](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mark+of+Mutiny) to steal the Huntmaster and attack. Nagai chose to block the [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) and dropping to 4 in the process. Aryabhima had likely chosen not to steal the [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) to avoid pushing its toughness high enough that a [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear) or [Flames of the Firebrand](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flames+of+the+Firebrand) wouldn't be able to kill it. Still, the damage may have been much more beneficial, especially when Nagai played his second [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) on the following turn.

Now at 9 and controlling three massive creatures, Nagai had a semblance of control. He wasn't quite willing to attack yet, fearing the attack back or another Mark, but that soon evaporated. Aryabhima added a [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) to his team, knocking Nagai down to 6, but that was as low as he would go. Nagai attacked with his team, signaling that he likely had a [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel). At the end of Aryabhima's turn, he indeed flashed the Angel in, giving him 5 more life and leaving him with three defenders. At this point, it was all Nagai's game, and Aryabhima knew it. After drawing his card, he chose not to waste any more time, conceding the game.

Rahman Aryabhima 0 - Mamoru Nagai 1

Aryabhima had another quick start, this time going from [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) to [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler), even managing to kill an [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) along the way. This would prevent Nagai from playing the second-turn [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter) that he had used to dispatch Kazumasa Satou in the Quarterfinals. Nagai had a [Pillar of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pillar+of+Flame) of his own, using it to kill the Cackler.

Aryabhima's [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) began its assault. Attacking for two a turn, Aryabhima sped his clock up with a [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger), knocking Nagai down to 11. Nagai slowed things down a touch with a [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) for one, but it only caused Aryabhima to pause before replaying it from his graveyard. The real hit came when he played [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells), going back up to 13. Aryabhima attacked in with his two creatures, and Nagai traded his Wolf away with the Messenger, which promptly returned for another 2 life. After combat, Aryabhima played a second Messenger, knocking Nagai to 7. Things seemed quite good for him

![](https://i.ormos.uk/b3/c4/b3c44e0552e833edcc717375ce45e7385bd64f72f1e17f25ad2eed95f6e9ab26.jpg)

When Nagai attacked for two on his turn, it was clear as crystal what he intended to do with his four mana. He simply passed the turn, letting his Huntmaster transform. This killed the freshly-cast Messenger, knocking Nagai to 5. Then, in Aryabhima's attack, Nagai used a clearly-telegraphed [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel) to reset the Huntmaster, gaining 2 life and another creature. The Angel traded with one of the Messengers, while the Wolf sacrificed himself to block the other. On the next turn, Nagai was forced to lose his [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells), dropping to 4.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/09/8d/098d2bf3f96e13740c51decdf603c8e0a97e130979c86dfe1a3bf2e31bb92de4.jpg)

Aryabhima let out a massive sigh as Nagai tapped five for a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk). Even with Nagai at 9 life, the [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) had an immense potential to steal the game for Nagai. That sigh of disbelief quickly changed to a sigh of relief, as Aryabhima top-decked a [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat), giving him just enough attack damage to seal the game before Nagai could gain any more life.

Rahman Aryabhima 1 - Mamoru Nagai 1

Aryabhima once again began with a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), giving him the first creature on the board. Nagai, meanwhile, cultivated his mana with a [Farseek](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Farseek). Aryabhima's [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) allowed the [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) to attack for 3, but it was the only attack it would get. Nagai cast a [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) for one, clearing the board. When he passed the turn, it became clear that this wouldn't be exactly like the last games had been: Nagai was missing his fourth land.

Aryabhima pressed. He easily found a third black source, dropping a [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) into play. Nagai fell to 15. [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter) provided an impressive wall for Nagai, but [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) allowed the Messenger to attack for four, offering the trade. Nagai sat pensively as he considered his options. Realizing shortly thereafter that Aryabhima had forgotten to announce his exalted trigger, Nagai blocked and called over the judge. The judge indeed ruled that Aryabhima's trigger was missed, and the 3/2 [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger) died to the [Smite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smite)r, much to Aryabhima's chagrin. After combat, Aryabhima added a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) and [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler) before passing the turn.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/6d/0b/6d0b2b86909d9af415b155589dbddc92a3f49fc261721179e1035f2015e14a75.jpg)

Nagai was still without a fourth land. All he could do was create more brick walls to buy him time, adding a second [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter) to his team. Aryabhima was clearly upset about having been called for failing to announce his trigger, but it appeared that he was being given another chance. A [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) hit the table, attacking in alongside the unblockable [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), dropping Nagai to 7. Nagai thumbed through his hand after drawing, gradually putting a freshly-drawn land into play. Facing four guaranteed points through the air and four other creatures, Nagai eventually decided to add a third [Smite](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Smite)r and an [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim) to his side. Aryabhima decided to simply send his [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat), this time announcing his exalted trigger, and Nagai dropped to 2.

Nagai had his back against the wall. [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) negated the last Aristocrat attack, but he was simply going to drop back down to 2 on the next attack. In order to cast [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk), Nagai had to tap his [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim), leaving him four untapped blockers.

Four was exactly the right number, as Aryabhima untapped and cast one of the best sideboard cards I have seen in Standard: [Crippling Blight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crippling+Blight). The one-mana enchantment is ideal for dealing with pesky mana creatures, [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat)s, or, in this case, preventing a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) from being able to block. With the [Blight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blight) in place, Aryabhima had one more attacker than Nagai could handle, sending him to the finals with a chance to make history.
