* * *

## Semifinals – Yuuji Okita (Human Reanimator) vs. Ryuji Murae (Red Black Aggro)

*by Steve Sadin*

 

Okita's Human Reanimator deck has turned more than a few heads this weekend. And understandably so. How is anyone's head supposed to remain completely affixed when they see a [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) deck tearing up the tournament?

Ryuji Murae's deck might not be anywhere near as earth shatteringly original as Okita's, but his take on Red Black Aggro is still sufficiently different from most Red Black decks to warrant notice. Murae's deck actually shares more in common with Mono-Red decks (he's only splashing black for [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), and [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat)) than it does with the Black Red Zombies decks that have been making their presence felt at American and European events over the past few weeks.

### Game One

Both players mulliganed to start their Semifinal match - but while Murae was content to stick with his six card hand, Okita had to go down to five.

Murae opened with a [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler), and a [Stromkirk Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stromkirk+Noble) – while Okita first play was an [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) that immediately blocked Murae's [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler), and killed off his [Stromkirk Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stromkirk+Noble) in combat.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/ea/9e/ea9e00cf927de61dc6e32aa4cc8e4743e78f965ec9b0ccb704328c0bd2dff31e.jpg)

Murae had a [Pillar of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pillar+of+Flame) to finish off the [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster), and then kept the pressure firmly on by playing a [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider), and immediately attacking with both of his creatures a turn later.

A new [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) jumped in front of the [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler), but Okita still fell to 11 from the attack.

And while a [Zealous Conscripts](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Zealous+Conscripts) allowed Okita to drag himself back into the game for a moment (by giving himself a blocker that was capable of trading with [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) and untapping his own [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) to kill off Murae's [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler)) – that moment wouldn't last as a miracled [Bonfire of the Damned](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Bonfire+of+the+Damned) for three, and a [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) gave Murae a quick one game lead.

Ryuji Murae 1 – Yuuji Okita 0

### Game Two

Okita kept his hand in the second game, but Murae again had to mulligan. Murae kept his 6 card hand and opened with a [Duress](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Duress) that stripped away a [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting), and left Okita with a hand of [Nightshade Peddler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Nightshade+Peddler), [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm), double [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells), but no blue lands.

It was clear to Murae that it was going to take Okita a while to do anything of note. Unfortunately for Murae, however, he had no way to capitalize on this.

A third turn [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler) gave Murae his first threat of the game – and while he was able to kill off an [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) with a [Searing Spear](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Searing+Spear), he had no answer for the [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) that Okita followed it up with.

When Murae took his assault to the air with a [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) he seemed to be very much back in the game...

But without any good spells to follow it up with, Murae could only sit and watch as his situation went from bad to worse. And when Okita was finally able to kill off his Murae's [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) by transforming Huntmaster into a [Ravager of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ravager+of+the+Fells), Murae knew he was finished.

Ryuji Murae 1 – Yuuji Okita 1

### Game Three

Despite the fact that Murae's deck is known for its aggression, he didn't play a single threat before casting a [Hellrider](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Hellrider) on turn 5 in the deciding game.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/3a/6d/3a6dee295f5af80b8e5c1941c322708359eab306e36a773b201bb4b82a3a7cb8.jpg)

This proved to be far too slow, as a [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding), and a [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) allowed Okita to [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) an Angel of Glory's Rise that brought back a pair of Huntmasters, a [Cathedral Sanctifier](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cathedral+Sanctifier), and an [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) along with it.

And while Murae had some burn spells to help buy a few turns, it was only a matter of time before Okita found himself in the finals.

Ryuji Murae 1 – Yuuji Okita 2

* * *

## Finals – Yuuji Okita vs. Rahman Aryabhima

*by Nate Price*

 

This finals was going to be a test for Yuuji Okita. Speaking with him earlier in the day about his deck, he revealed that he wasn't too pleased with his matchup against Zombies. My initial reaction was, "Umm...if you don't think you play well against Zombies, why would you ever play that deck?" My second was, "You've only lost one match...clearly you must be doing something right."

Now, he's sitting in the finals of Grand Prix Nagoya, one match away from taking the title, and he has to face the deck he didn't want to play against. Honestly, I can't imagine how many people out there are openly rooting for Okita to take home this title and prevent BR Zombies from winning yet another Grand Prix. I'd imagine it's at least one or two. But Okita doesn't like his chances, even after beating Zombies three times throughout the tournament, he still doesn't like it.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/24/97/2497cc28675fb3f6cb57efe92c349ceb5123586ec49d72b9cc29db385dbd8e30.jpg)

Aryabhima, on the other hand, is packing a Zombie deck built to beat other Zombie decks, not Reanimator decks. While his deck is very well-tuned against the rest of the field, he might not be able to handle the one-shot fireworks of this combo Reanimator deck. Still, Zombies always has a massively fast core, so his speed may be enough to slip under the [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites).

Aryabhima started off with a [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul), immediately following that with a [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler). Okita found a piece of his engine early, putting a Constant [Flood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flood)ing on a [Rootbound Crag](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rootbound+Crag). [Blood Artist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Artist) came down for Aryabhima, and he attacked for four, dropping Okita to 14.

Okita began to dig. Constant [Flood](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Flood)ing sent three cards to his graveyard, while [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch) sent four more. A [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) finished his turn off, sending yet two more to his graveyard. In all, Okita had put four creatures into his graveyard, including an [Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise), but there was no [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites). He had a copy in his hand, but he wasn't playing any black sources. It had to hit the graveyard.

Aryabhima kept attacking, and Okita fell to 10. He didn't have a third land, so his options were quite limited. Despite a good start, Aryabhima was not in a great spot when Okita cast [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells). Okita's two creatures allowed him to block and trade away with Aryabhima's creatures on the next turn. While the [Blood Artist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Artist) knocked Okita back down to 8, he was perilously close to stabilizing with an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites). After losing his attackers, Aryabhima cast a [Geralf's Messenger](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Geralf%27s+Messenger), dropping Okita to 6, and passed the turn.

This was it, the turn that Okita had to make his move. The turn that he always feared would never work out his way. He began by tapping his lands for mana, dumping more cards into his graveyard. The very last one to hit his graveyard was the [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) he needed to win the game. He cast it on [Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise), bringing back the following creatures:

![](https://i.ormos.uk/18/84/1884bdccc3d2d55f6947580de3acc7814048e5e2a4108b3fa2df71e9b15eff10.jpg)

Okita stacked the triggers such that he could steal Aryabhima's [Blood Artist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Artist) with his Conscripts before the Commander's triggers resolved, giving him three large creatures with haste. Seven other creatures hit play that turn, giving each of Okita's attacker +7/+7. With that much damage, Okita was able to end the game in one fell swoop, dealing exactly one more damage than necessary to kill Aryabhima.

Yuuji Okita 1 - Rahman Aryabhima 0

For someone who didn't like his chances against Zombies, his first game against it in the finals was fairly impressive. His sideboard gave him even more cards that would help him against Zombies. [Cathedral Sanctifier](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cathedral+Sanctifier) provides life and a blocker, [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm) provides additional removal, and [Rolling Temblor](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rolling+Temblor) can wipe the board long enough for Okita to go off. Aryabhima isn't without his own tricks, as his [Deathrite Shaman](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Deathrite+Shaman) is just bonkers against Reanimator, as is the graveyard-removing power of [Rakdos Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Charm).

Aryabhima began with a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), exactly as expected from his deck. A [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) joined it on the next turn, and Aryabhima attacked for three. Okita began digging on his second turn with a [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting). An [Angel of Glory's Rise](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Glory%27s+Rise) hit the graveyard, giving Aryabhima a look into his future. Undaunted, Aryabhima did all he could, attacking for four and dropping Okita to 13. After combat, he added a [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul) and passed the turn. Okita had no play on his turn, simply passing back to Aryabhima.

And then it was over. With Okita at 13, a [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) came down, swinging in for ten damage. Okita hadn't managed to get any more cards in his graveyard, so there was no way that he was going to be able to actually stop Aryabhima on his next turn. Rather than draw things out, Okita simply conceded, less than three minutes into the game. So much for needing sideboard cards.

Yuuji Okita 1 - Rahman Aryabhima 1

One more match and something great would happen, regardless of the outcome. Either Okita would win, wresting a third title in four events from the hands of Zombies, or Aryabhima would win and join Brian Kibler, Alex Shvartsman, and Martin Juza (though he might as well be Japanese) as the only non-Japanese players to win an individual Japanese Grand Prix. It's an exclusive group, and joining the ranks is an impressive notch on any player's belt.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/77/7f/777f458cf2c2cba8bacc7b14e430d6555699a625d6bdd6cc4bcda93ef8575a94.jpg)

Okita was on the play in the final game, benefiting him greatly by giving him a step ahead of the aggressive Zombies deck. Both players mulliganed, and Okita did not look happy with his second hand. After putting his face in his hands, he gasped a breath before deciding to go back for five. He looked slightly relieved when Aryabhima followed suit.

Interestingly, neither player's deck is particularly affected by mulligans. Obviously, they have less cards to play with, but each is capable of more than adequate draws, even with only five cards. Okita has [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch) and [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) to get his engine going, and he doesn't care if his cards like [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm) and [Faithless Looting](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Faithless+Looting) simply mill him. Aryabhima, on the other hand, only needs two lands and three creatures to get the ball rolling, something his deck can easily cough up.

Aryabhima began with a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) and [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), while Okita started with the aforementioned [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch). Following that with a [Cathedral Sanctifier](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Cathedral+Sanctifier)s gave Okita a bit more breathing room, though they wouldn't be able to block the [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy). Aryabhima attacked with the Knight, and Okita showed off his other great sideboard card: [Izzet Charm](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Charm). The Knight died, and Okita stayed safely at 18. Aryabhima added a second [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) to his team and passed the turn.

Okita made a [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) on his turn, drawing exactly the cards he needed to make his win from five cards a reality. Unfortunately, Aryabhima was doing quite well himself, ramping all the way up to [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat). Okita traded his Sanctifier for a [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) and dropped to 12.

![](https://i.ormos.uk/00/ab/00abcf0fae4d974a48f2c98aa8e548324fc361e69ed91845b56a1c99e37790d5.jpg)

Facing a very quick death, Okita needed to get there quickly. His earlier [Mulch](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Mulch) had put an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) and a [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) into his graveyard, giving him some potential to stall a little longer. But who wants to stall when you can just win? [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) put an [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) into his graveyard, and the Angel came into play, along with an army of buddies. Between the lifegain and the ridiculous number of creatures that just came into play, Okita was hilariously safe from harm. Aryabhima wisely sacrificed his [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler) to his Aristocrat in response to the Angel hitting play, but Okita's new [Izzet Staticaster](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Izzet+Staticaster) killed it during Aryabhima's upkeep. With nothing left in play and facing the comical assortment of creatures on Okita's side, Aryabhima conceded the final game, making Yuuji Okita the Grand Prix Nagoya 2012 Champion!

* * *

## Top 5 Cards

*by Steve Sadin and Nate Price*

 

 

![Crippling Blight](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Crippling+Blight)

 

### 5\. [Crippling Blight](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Crippling+Blight)

In a format that changes as swiftly as Standard appears to, it's always imperative to stay on top of the latest tech. This little gem came out of the sideboard of Finalist Rahman Aryabhima's BR Zombies deck.

This card kills the following creatures found in the Top 16 of Grand Prix Nagoya: [Arbor Elf](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Arbor+Elf), [Avacyn's Pilgrim](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Avacyn%27s+Pilgrim), [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy), [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat), [Snapcaster Mage](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Snapcaster+Mage), [Blood Artist](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Artist), and [Stonewright](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonewright). That alone makes it a reasonable card, but why is it better than [Pillar of Flame](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Pillar+of+Flame), which exiles creatures, or [Tragic Slip](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Tragic+Slip), which is an instant?

Simple. Play it on a [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter) or a [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk) and your opponent can't block with them. This seemingly innocuous line of text is ridiculously important against the green decks in the field, which look to present creatures that the Zombie decks simply can't attack through.

This simple spell completely wrecks combat math, and it single-handedly won Aryabhima his final semifinal game. I spent all weekend looking for tech, and this gem escaped my notice until the last minute. Now, I've made sure it won't slip yours.

  
  

 

![Knight of Infamy](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Knight+of+Infamy)

 

### 4\. [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy)

Between [Diregraf Ghoul](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Diregraf+Ghoul), [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler), [Rakdos Cackler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Rakdos+Cackler), [Stromkirk Noble](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stromkirk+Noble), [Stonewright](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Stonewright), and [Reckless Waif](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Reckless+Waif) there are a lot of good aggressive one mana creatures in red, and black right now. And depending on what kind of a deck you want to run, then you can make a valid argument for including (or not including) any of these creatures.

However, when it comes to two mana creatures, you don't have quite as much room to maneuver.

If you're playing an aggressive Red-Black deck that you absolutely have to play [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy).

The fact that it has protection from white allows you to attack with impunity past [Restoration Angel](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Restoration+Angel)s, and [Loxodon Smiter](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Loxodon+Smiter)s -- and the fact that it has exalted allows you to send your [Ash Zealot](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Ash+Zealot)s or your [Gravecrawler](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Gravecrawler)s (depending on what kind of a deck you're running) into [Thragtusk](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Thragtusk)s without any fear of retribution.

So the next time you sit down to build a Red Black deck, make sure that you start with 4 [Blood Crypt](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Blood+Crypt), 4 [Dragonskull Summit](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Dragonskull+Summit), and 4 [Knight of Infamy](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Knight+of+Infamy) before you do anything else.

  
  

 

![Huntmaster of the Fells](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells)

 

### 3\. [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells)

A few short weeks after [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) was printed, it propelled Paulo Vitor Damo da Rosa and Brian Kibler into the Finals of Pro Tour *Dark Ascension*.

Nearly a year later, [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) continues to make its presence felt -- playing a pivotal role in decks like Mamoru Nagai's Naya concoction that can accelerate it into play on turn three, and in Grand Prix Nagoya Champion Yuuji Okita's Human Reanimator (where it's a threat, a way to buy time, and a combo piece).

So the next time that you think that [Huntmaster of the Fells](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Huntmaster+of+the+Fells) is going to go quietly into the night, you should remember that that's exactly where Werewolves thrive.

  
  

 

![Unburial Rites](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Unburial+Rites)

 

### 2\. [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites)

If Thragrtusk, and [Falkenrath Aristocrat](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Falkenrath+Aristocrat) are the two most important creatures in Standard right now -- then I think it's safe to say that [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites) is the most important spell.

Between control decks like the Four Color Value Rites deck that Conley Woods piloted to a Top 4 finish at Grand Prix San Antonio, Reanimator decks like Kenji Tsumura's [Angel of Serenity](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Angel+of+Serenity) deck, and Combo Reanimator decks like the one that Yuuji Okita used to win this very tournament -- it's clear that there are plenty of good ways to build your deck around [Unburial Rites](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Unburial+Rites).

So if you think that you can just ignore graveyard based strategies in Standard, then you're sadly mistaken.

  
  

 

![Chronic Flooding](http://gatherer.wizards.com/Handlers/Image.ashx?type=card&name=Chronic+Flooding)

 

### 1\. [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding)

The most play I've seen [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) get prior to this weekend was as a proxy card. Now, it's gone and won itself a Grand Prix.

Described by Yuuji Okita as the best card in his deck, [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) provides an immense number of cards milled for a very minimal investment. Two mana gets you three cards per land tapped.

If you play it on turn two, you are likely going to tap that land three more times, milling a whopping nine cards in the process. In the last game of the finals, where both Okita and his opponent mulliganned to five, it was [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) that provided him a steady stream of cards in his graveyard, leading ultimately to his victory.

I watched most of Okita's games on Day 2, and I don't think he lost a single game where he resolved a [Chronic Flooding](https://web.archive.org/web/20210503041739/http://gatherer.wizards.com/Pages/Card/Details.aspx?name=Chronic+Flooding) on turn two or three. It's simply the best bang for your buck, as far as milling goes. All this from a card that has seen more use as a coaster than a **Magic** card.

Nice find.

  
  

* * *
